# Gate G1 — identifiable question v3 (for sol-high G1 re-review round 3)

Addresses r2 verdict (70 FAIL): ONE coherent question, external endpoint specified NOW with schema-demonstrated join, no alternating among mean-shift / ranking / bound framings.

## The single question
**Does measured format-bundle lability of a gene's dependency (Broad controlled panel) predict how badly that gene's dependency ranking transfers to independent Sanger organoids — or can any such association be bounded below decision relevance?**

## Locked-at-G1 definitions (Lock 1 finalizes numerics, not identity)
- **Lability (predictor, Broad Layer-A, frozen values):** for gene g, `L_g` = equal-weight mean over the **6 format-contrast models** of (mean over that model's pairs of **|Chronos_Dome − Chronos_Plastic|**), medium held fixed within pair. Bundle = Matrigel-dome vs plastic. (Operator per sol-xhigh unstick; no within-model cancellation.)
- **External transfer loss (response, Sanger Layer-C):** cohort = **148 organoids / 144 donors** (CRISPR_available, donor-level Broad-disjoint via `overlap_Broad_DepMap` at donor level; independently matches S3's 148/144). Outcome source = Supplementary Table 6 long-format corrected LFCs by `sample_ID` (schema-verified: 162 samples, all 148 eligible present; values quarantined in-zip until Lock-2). Library rule (Lock 1): minLib primary; 16 dual-library models → v1_1 sensitivity. Direction: more negative LFC = stronger dependency (verify against S2 control-gene docs at Lock 1, schema-level).
  For organoid o and gene g: within-organoid rank `R_og` of LFC across the confirmatory gene set (rank 1 = strongest dependency). Consensus 2D rank `R0_g` from Broad `AdherentMean` (rank 1 = most negative/strongest). **Per-gene transfer loss:** `D_g = mean_o |R_og − R0_g| / (G−1)` (normalized mean absolute rank displacement, 0 = perfect rank transfer). Donor-equal weighting (organoids of same donor averaged first).
- **Confirmatory gene set (schema-demonstrated):** **207 genes** = development-primary 216 ∩ Table-6-present (9 dropped by Sanger coverage: ACTR2, ARHGAP11A, CDC42, CTNNA1, FMNL3, ITGA11, PYROXD1, TERB2, TLN1; list frozen in `data/confirmatory_gene_candidates.json`). Development-primary 216 = 250 − 26 Tier-A selection-tail − TMOD (unadjudicated alias; sensitivity-only as TMOD1/7111) − 7 low-reference-N (AdherentN<400: CYFIP2, DEXI, DOCK1, OR14K1, PPP2R2D at N=9; PARVA, PKD1 at N=145).
- **Primary confirmatory parameter:** partial Spearman association between `L_g` and `D_g` across the 207 genes, adjusting baseline strength |AdherentMean| — defined **exactly** as Pearson correlation of rank residuals (each variable ranked, linearly residualized on ranked |AdherentMean|). Uncertainty: donor-cluster bootstrap over 144 donors (external side) × model-cluster bootstrap over 6 Broad models (lability side), joint propagation; pathway-block sensitivity. Evaluated **once** at Lock 2.
- **Development analogue (exploratory only, never confirmatory):** same construction with Broad `|MeanDifference|` replacing `D_g` — used for policy-loop tasks and feasibility. It is a descriptive cohort-shift association, **not** the transfer claim. Current exploratory value (v2 operator, 216 genes): partial ρ = 0.055, model-bootstrap 95% CI [−0.086, 0.162] (`src/precision_feasibility.py`, regenerable).

## Decision rules (disjoint; two-sided bound)
- `SIGNAL`: confirmatory 95% CI for partial ρ entirely **above +δ**.
- `BOUND` (informative refutation): CI entirely **within (−δ, +δ)** — excludes decision-relevant association in *both* directions.
- `INCONCLUSIVE`: otherwise. Reported as such; no reinterpretation.
**δ candidate = 0.20, justification (decision-linked, Lock 1 ratifies or replaces with stated reasoning):** the endpoint exists to decide whether lability should *triage target trust* (deprioritize high-lability targets from 2D evidence). A rank association of 0.20 corresponds to lability ordering agreeing with displacement ordering only marginally better than chance for pairwise target comparisons (Kendall-equivalent ≈ 0.13, ~57% pairwise concordance) — below any threshold at which a triage rule would change shortlist composition in practice. δ is *not* derived from our exploratory point estimate (0.055) or from S3's different correlation type; Lock 1 must re-justify or amend it with the same decision argument before outcomes are touched.

## Independence & preprocessing (concrete graph)
- Development artifacts (lability, exclusions, consensus ranks) derive **only** from Broad study-specific figshare files — no Sanger outcome fitted any of them (verified file-by-file; exposure log complete).
- Sanger processed LFCs were fitted **within the 930-model joint matrix** (CRISPRcleanR per-sample; spline + ComBat across samples/libraries, incl. 571 Broad traditional lines). This is within-cohort transductive coupling on the *evaluation* side; it does not flow into development artifacts. Lock-1 decision (pre-registered): primary analysis on author-processed LFCs with this coupling disclosed + sensitivity on development-only refit from figshare guide counts if feasible; if refit infeasible, claims carry the coupling limitation explicitly. "Downgrading" alone is never treated as repair for any actual cross-boundary flow — none is identified in the development direction; the Broad→Sanger processing direction (traditional lines inside ComBat) is disclosed and bounded by the refit sensitivity.
- Broad minipool Chronos: fitted within the minipool screens ("same manner as genome-wide"); paired contrasts share processing within pairs. Genome-wide NextGen Chronos used CNS-pretrained parameters — affects only the *development* transfer table, already demoted to descriptive.

## Novelty (bounded, corrected)
Not claimed: generic transfer prediction (S3), S1's class-level format/medium effects, heterogeneity-from-sign-flips (retracted everywhere incl. novelty audit v2). Claimed as plausibly open: a **selection-controlled, panel-conditional test — or precision-supported two-sided bound — of whether measured format lability predicts within-organoid rank-transfer loss in a donor-disjoint external cohort, beyond baseline dependency strength.** S1 never linked minipool lability to any external ranking endpoint; S3 never used measured environmental sensitivity. Absence-of-hit caveat stands; claim-by-claim audit vs S1 Fig. 5 analyses is in `reviews/novelty_audit_v2.md`.

## Feasibility statement
Environmental side: 6 models (donors unresolved → claims conditional on measured models; generalization to new models explicitly wider/not claimed). External side: 144 donors — per-gene displacement `D_g` is a donor-averaged statistic (stable); primary uncertainty dominated by the 6-model lability side (development CI width ≈0.25 for partial ρ). A BOUND verdict at δ=0.20 is precision-plausible **iff** the external estimate remains near the development one; SIGNAL at δ=0.20 would require a much larger external association — not promised. INCONCLUSIVE is a live possibility and an acceptable honest outcome.

## Layer-B honesty
No reserved biological evidence currently exists. Inspected Broad observations are Layer-A forever. Reserved policy evaluation (Phase 5) will use either (a) genuinely uninspected biological evidence reserved before inspection, or (b) be labeled **reused-data engineering evaluation** with C9 wording downgraded accordingly. Fresh rollouts generate new *engineering* outcomes on known biological rewards unless (a) holds.

# Provenance & identifiability audit v2 (2026-10-28) — supersedes v1 (G1 review 56/100 remediation)

All v1 factual errors corrected below are independently re-verified from primary files. Review: `reviews/g1_review_r1_verdict.md`.

## Environmental panel (Broad minipool) — corrected counts
- 18 screens in released Chronos matrix (18×1000, 0 missing) from **34 sequencing attempts** (35 rows in sequence metadata incl. pDNA reference); 16/18 screens attempted with technical duplicates; **1 replicate removed** (`0178_Adh_Nat_Serum_Dome_RepB`, low NNMD) — screen retained on remaining replicate. Observation-level table: `data/observation_provenance_minipool.csv` (35 rows: model, lineage where known, lab=Broad, library, medium, format, replicate, reads, in-matrix flag, attrition notes).
- **Contrast-specific independent support (corrected):** medium bundle (OPAC vs RPMI/FBS) = 5 pairs / **4 distinct models**; format bundle (Matrigel dome vs plastic) = 7 pairs / **6 distinct models**. `ACH-003040` contributes 2 pairs to each (only complete 4-condition model) — equal-pair weighting overweights it; Lock 1 defines model-level weighting. **Donor counts UNRESOLVED** for all 7 models (no donor field in released metadata); models ≠ verified independent donors.
- Condition coverage per model is sparse (2–4 of 4 cells); released metadata does **not** distinguish unattempted vs growth-failed vs QC-excluded for missing cells → recorded as "not in released matrix; reason unavailable". Zero matrix missingness ≠ complete experimental coverage.
- **Treatment bundles (named honestly):** medium = full OPAC↔RPMI/FBS composition change (not isolated serum); format = plastic↔Matrigel-dome (geometry + matrix exposure, not isolated geometry). Interpretations restricted to observed within-model bundle contrasts.
- **QC discrepancy in primary source (documented, unresolved):** S1 Methods state ≥500,000 reads across replicates; Results/Fig. 5 text states ≥5×10⁶ total readcounts + NNMD ≤ −1.5. Observed screen reads (6.7M–59M) satisfy both; no analysis impact identified, recorded for fidelity.
- Lineages (6/7): ACH-000048 ovarian endometrioid, ACH-000108 pancreas adenosquamous, ACH-000178 pancreatic adenocarcinoma, ACH-001418 serous ovarian, ACH-002958 stomach adenocarcinoma (organoid), ACH-003040 pancreatic adenocarcinoma (organoid). **ACH-003052 (organoid) lineage absent from all released Broad metadata** → excluded from any lineage-conditioned analysis until resolved; format contrasts (within-model) unaffected.

## Statistics — retraction
v1's "heterogeneity dominates / averages near-uninterpretable" is **retracted**. Sign-flip rates for test genes (medium 90.8%, format 98.4%) match 750 controls (92.1%, 98.1%) and zero-effect noise expectations. Magnitude evidence: median |Δ| medium 0.183 (test) vs 0.164 (controls); mean |mean Δ| 0.137 vs 0.094 — modestly larger test-gene effects, consistent with selection, not proof of heterogeneity. All numbers regenerable via `src/audit_minipool.py` → `data/identifiability_audit.json` (audit v2).

## Selection trace & circularity control
- Library (250 genes) designed from functional classes + the genome-wide 3D-vs-2D comparison: "200 outlier genes" = top 100 each tail of mean dependency difference excluding common essentials (S1 Fig. 5), enriched gene-set clusters then informed classes.
- **Tier-A exclusion (independently reproduced):** 26/250 panel genes are direct 200-outlier members → excluded from primary analyses (`data/selection_exclusions.json`), leaving 224; 1 further attrition (`TMOD` absent from transfer table) → **223 joined genes**.
- Residual pathway/class-level selection cannot be excluded per-gene. **All claims are conditional on the fixed selected panel; no generalization to unselected genes.** Broad-side associations are development/descriptive evidence only.

## Preprocessing dependency graphs (audited from source text/code)
- **Broad genome-wide Chronos:** all NextGen screens used Humagne library → library batch **confounded** with organoid-ness; authors pre-trained Chronos on traditional + NextGen **CNS** models, then applied parameters to organoids. Consequence: NextGen Chronos values (incl. the organoids-vs-2D table, n=94 vs 433) are not outcome-independent of NextGen data; usable as **development** evidence, never as independent validation of Broad-derived selections. Minipool Chronos computed "in the same manner" on enAsCas12a screens (separate library); within-model paired contrasts share processing within pairs.
- **Sanger:** pipeline = CRISPRcleanR (per-sample CN correction) → spline normalization (expression bias) → **ComBat batch correction across samples** → BAGEL2 binary calls (verified in `Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R`). ComBat/spline parameters are fitted across the cohort's outcomes → processed Sanger matrices are **not** development-independent by construction. Authoritative processed route per S2: Cell Model Passports + DepMap Miner; figshare holds figure-reproduction files incl. `07_EssMatrix_qnorm_corrected_logFCs.RData` (continuous LFCs; **not downloaded**) and BAGEL binary depletions (downloaded, quarantined; 17,647×930 ACH — a DepMap-wide binary matrix, **not** itself the 162-organoid continuous outcome). Confirmatory use requires, at Lock 1/2: cohort identity via crosswalk, duplicate-library rule (2 libraries/model in places), and either development-only refit from counts (`sgRNAs-counts…csv` exists on figshare) or explicit downgrade of independence claims. Binary BAGEL ≠ continuous rank endpoint; never substituted silently.
- Assay scales (Chronos vs corrected LFC) remain non-interchangeable; cross-study = rank/calibration only.

## Exposure adjudication (final)
Glimpsed cells (from retained transcript, file not reopened): genes `A1BG, A1CF, A2M` × models `ACH-001421, ACH-001412, ACH-000888, ACH-001184, ACH-000317, ACH-000264`. All 6 models are **traditional 2D cell lines** (Broad metadata, `IsNextGen=False`); none of the 3 genes is in the 250 panel. **Zero candidate Layer-C organoid outcomes were exposed.** Disposition: organoid-cohort blinding intact with permanent disclosure (`data/exposure_log.jsonl`); no exclusions required; BAGEL file stays quarantined; any future use of those 6 cell lines as development data must cite this log.

## Partitions (corrected)
Layer A = Broad study-specific figshare (inspected; development). Layer C candidates = Sanger outcomes (quarantined; schema-only). **Layer B is NOT carved from inspected minipool outcomes** — B panels are *task definitions for fresh Phase-5 rollouts whose outcomes do not yet exist*; any policy evaluation reusing already-inspected observations is labeled **reused-data engineering evaluation**, not reserved/untouched. Exposure history ships with every split manifest.

## Remaining G1-open (honest)
Donor identities for the 7 minipool models + HCM-SANG↔ACH crosswalk (routes: DepMap Model.csv interactive download; S3 Zenodo registries at Phase 2); genome-wide Broad matrix (interactive portal route); Sanger continuous-matrix identity confirmation via Passports/Miner docs. None blocks the narrowed development estimand in `reviews/g1_question_v2.md`; each gates the specific claims noted there.

**G1 verdict: FAIL.** V2 establishes a computable, panel-conditional **descriptive association**, but still does not establish the transfer question it claims to test. This failure concerns identification and evidence—not unfinished Lock-1 implementation or the broader project’s completion criteria.

I read all requested materials. Independent Broad checks reproduced the matrix dimensions, contrast counts, selection exclusions, sign-flip statistics, direction checks, and raw correlation. 1. **R1-1 [CRITICAL] Operational estimand — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Question v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/g1_question_v2.md:10) now defines `T_g = |MeanDifference_g|` and a partial Spearman association. The raw association reproduces exactly: **ρ = 0.1668304275**.

   However, absolute organoid–adherent **mean dependency difference is not loss of predictive transfer**. A relationship can transfer perfectly despite a mean shift, or fail without one. The external endpoint remains “**continuous corrected LFC ranking endpoint, exact form at Lock 1**.” Its reference, comparator, and numerical transfer-loss quantity are still unspecified. Those are identification choices, not merely implementation details.

2. **R1-2 [CRITICAL] Selection circularity — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:18) reports “**26/250 panel genes are direct 200-outlier members**.” I independently reproduced the exact 26-gene exclusion list using the specified tails.

   The restriction “**Broad-side associations are development/descriptive evidence only**” is appropriate. Nevertheless, pathway-level selection remains, and the primary response still comes from the comparison that informed panel construction. Excluding direct tail members does not make the remaining association selection-independent. Circularity is adequately contained **for a descriptive development statistic**, but not resolved for the claimed independent transfer finding. Donor-disjoint external evidence remains unestablished.

3. **R1-3 [CRITICAL] Preprocessing independence — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   The audit now identifies concrete transformations, but its conclusion that Sanger matrices are “**not development-independent by construction**” is too strong. The [author pipeline](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/main/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R) computes cross-sample mean LFCs for spline correction and applies ComBat across libraries. This establishes outcome-dependent coupling within the processed cohort; it does **not**, by itself, establish influence on Broad development artifacts.

   The missing evidence remains the actual fitting populations and artifact dependencies across the development/evaluation boundary. Conversely, merely “downgrading independence” cannot repair genuine cross-boundary leakage. The Broad minipool processing description also remains insufficient to identify its fitted parameter dependencies.

4. **R1-4 [HIGH] Independent-unit counts and weighting — RESOLVED for the counting error.**  
   [The audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/identifiability_audit.json:1) correctly reports **5 medium pairs/4 models** and **7 format pairs/6 models**. Both reproduce. Question v2 explicitly averages ACH-003040’s two contrasts before assigning equal model weights.

   “**Donors … UNRESOLVED**” appropriately prevents equating six models with six verified independent donors. Donor ascertainment remains an external-eligibility requirement, but the original counting and weighting mistake is corrected.

5. **R1-5 [HIGH] Unsupported heterogeneity interpretation — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:14) explicitly says “**heterogeneity dominates / averages near-uninterpretable … retracted**.” The test/control sign-flip and magnitude statistics reproduce.

   Yet the still-operative [novelty audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/novelty_audit_v1.md:15) continues to assert “**per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity**,” using the same sign flips to motivate context stratification. The correction has not propagated through the package. Technical-noise calibration also remains absent.

6. **R1-6 [HIGH] Observation provenance and attrition — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   The new [35-row registry](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/observation_provenance_minipool.csv:1) materially improves replicate, condition, QC, and laboratory documentation. Missing lineage for ACH-003052 is explicitly acknowledged and appropriately limits lineage-conditioned claims.

   However, donor identities and biological sample origins remain incomplete, and missing condition combinations are not individually registered with their disposition. More seriously, “**1 replicate removed**” conflicts with the released sequence metadata, which flags **two** excluded replicates:

   - `0178_Adh_Alt_SF_Dome_RepA`
   - `0178_Adh_Nat_Serum_Dome_RepB`

   The registry copies both exclusion flags but annotates only the latter. Screen-level read totals include both flagged replicates’ reads. This requires reconciliation of QC summaries versus actual scoring inputs; it does not establish which replicates entered Chronos.

7. **R1-7 [HIGH] Treatment scope and confounding — RESOLVED.**  
   [V2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:9) correctly names the full medium-composition and plastic–Matrigel-dome bundles. Question v2 explicitly rejects “**causal explanation of transfer failure**” and isolated geometry/serum claims. Restricting interpretation to observed within-model comparisons is appropriate.

8. **R1-8 [HIGH] Sanger validation outcome identity — UNRESOLVED.**  
   The package now correctly distinguishes binary BAGEL calls from continuous outcomes. But [provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:23) still says the continuous matrix is “**not downloaded**,” while cohort identity, crosswalks, coverage, and duplicate-library disposition remain future work.

   Naming Passports/Miner and locating a continuous-file entry establishes a plausible route, not a demonstrated joinable evaluation cohort. Schema and identifier verification can proceed without inspecting quarantined outcome values; it has not yet been supplied.

9. **R1-9 [HIGH] Exposure disposition — RESOLVED for the documented 18-cell event.**  
   [The exposure log](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/exposure_log.jsonl:4) now names the three genes and six models. Independent Broad metadata checks confirm all six models have `IsTraditional2D=True`; none of the three genes belongs to the 250-gene panel.

   That supports retaining the candidate organoid evaluation rather than discarding the resource. It supports the narrow conclusion that the **listed exposed cells are outside the eligible target panel**. It does not certify that public outcomes were globally unknowable or that an as-yet-unresolved cohort is genuinely blinded.

10. **R1-10 [HIGH] Layer-B reuse — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    [Partitions v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/partitions_provisional.json:8) correctly labels inspected-data reuse “**reused-data engineering evaluation**.”

    However, “**task definitions for FRESH Phase-5 rollouts**” does not itself establish reserved biological evidence. New rollouts can generate new engineering outcomes while reusing known biological rewards. No actual reserved panel membership is demonstrated. This remains a policy-evaluation limitation, not an independent reason to fail the scientific G1 gate.

11. **R1-11 [HIGH] Precise novelty — UNRESOLVED.**  
    The [novelty audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/novelty_audit_v1.md:13) still says “**Nobody has tested**,” retains the rejected heterogeneity rationale, and lists supplement/code reads as “**Open items for G1 gate**.”

    More fundamentally, its proposed gap concerns environmental sensitivity versus **transfer degradation**, whereas the implemented statistic concerns environmental measurements versus **cohort mean differences**. That narrower descriptive relationship needs its own comparison against S1’s analyses. The current audit does not establish it as a distinct unresolved contribution.

12. **R1-12 [HIGH] Falsification, precision, and δ — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    The `SIGNAL`/`BOUND`/`INCONCLUSIVE` rules are substantially clearer. The [precision artifact](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/precision_feasibility_v1.json:14) honestly states that its model bootstrap captures “**environmental-side uncertainty only**.”

    Nevertheless:

    - The supplied interval **[0.00834, 0.24742] concerns raw ρ**, while the primary parameter is partial ρ.
    - It straddles δ = 0.20 and establishes neither a signal nor a bound.
    - No eligible external donor count or external-endpoint precision calculation is supplied.
    - Interval width alone does not establish that a meaningful signal is infeasible.
    - A bound below **+0.20** excludes a sufficiently large positive association; it does not establish weak association in both directions.

    **δ = 0.20 may remain a candidate for Lock 1, but its present justification is inadequate.** “Would not change target-trust decisions” lacks a specified decision rule. S3’s prediction–outcome correlations measure something different from this across-gene explanatory association. Finalizing δ later is permissible; treating its scientific relevance or attainability as established now is not.

13. **R1-13 [MEDIUM] Manifest completeness and reproducibility — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    Both formerly omitted Sanger files are now manifested. Saved authoritative responses include version and license information, and all 18 local files match their records. The revised audit code independently reproduces the reported direction checks and descriptive statistics.

    Remaining gaps include missing per-entry article versions for **9/13 Broad entries**, unpinned processing-code revisions, and no supplied regeneration procedure for the precision artifact. The documented QC discrepancy is useful, but the stated screen-read range “**6.7M–59M**” is incorrect: the released maximum is **88,316,766**.

The additional issues are consequential:

- **The claimed common reference population is inaccurate.** Among the 223 joined genes, 215 have `AdherentN=433`; five have **9**, two have **145**, and one has **432**. Thus the primary response mixes differently supported reference comparisons. Eligibility and uncertainty must address this explicitly.
- **The claimed TMOD attrition is not established.** The table contains `TMOD1 (7111)`, and [NCBI documents `TMOD` as its alias](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7111/). The library guides require identifier adjudication before calling this missing outcome coverage. **223 exact-symbol joins is verified; 223 as the scientifically correct eligible population is provisional.**
- **The lability operator changed from the unstick recommendation.** Unstick averages absolute medium-specific changes; v2 takes the absolute value after averaging those changes within a model. These differ for **111/223 genes**, allowing opposing changes in ACH-003040 to cancel. Either can define a descriptive score, but they answer different questions.
- **“Partial Spearman” needs an exact definition.** The reported **0.085695** reproduces by correlating re-ranked rank residuals. Pearson correlation of the rank residuals gives **0.083961**. This small discrepancy is suitable for Lock-1 clarification, but the accompanying variance-explained argument must match the chosen operation.
- **The fallback changes the question again.** A “within-Broad environmental bound” does not follow automatically from an upper bound on association with absolute cohort mean differences. It cannot bound the causal contribution of culture or latent biological sensitivity.

**Operational and joinable:** yes, for the narrowly defined descriptive Broad statistic, subject to identifier adjudication and heterogeneous reference coverage. **Circularity:** appropriately disclosed for development description, inadequately controlled for independent confirmation. **Precision:** limitations are disclosed, but primary and external feasibility remain undemonstrated. **δ:** provisional, with insufficient decision justification.

A G1 pass does not require completed external evaluation or finalized Lock-1 machinery. It does require one coherent, plausibly novel question with a demonstrated evidence route. V2 still alternates among cohort mean shift, unspecified ranking transfer, and an environmental bound. The [unstick review](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/reviews/unstick_g1.md:15) explicitly warned: “**A correlation between minipool effects and the downloaded Broad group-comparison table … cannot clear the independent-transfer gate.**” V2 has implemented that development correlation without resolving the distinction.

5 - Data and identifiability audit

Before adaptive hypothesis search, construct a provenance table with biological model, donor, tumor lineage, sample origin, laboratory, assay library, medium, growth format, replicate identity, release, and measured outcome.

Create explicit crosswalks among Broad, HCMI, Sanger, and author-specific identifiers. Mark uncertain donor identity as uncertain rather than assuming independence.

Determine which observations are truly paired. Count independent biological models and donors. Do not count genes, guide RNAs, wells, alternative cultures, or technical replicates as additional independent patients.

Audit release composition and preprocessing provenance. An integrated release may contain validation observations or corrections learned jointly from them. Removing overlapping rows alone does not undo leakage through outcome-guided target selection, normalization, or model selection. Prefer compatible study-specific releases and transformations fitted on development data. If a fitted correction used external dependency outcomes, rebuild a development-only analysis or obtain genuinely independent validation evidence. Disclosure alone does not restore independence. Distinguish that outcome leakage from covariate-only joint preprocessing, which must be disclosed as transductive feature preprocessing with appropriately limited claims. This is a required provenance check, not an assertion that any named release is contaminated.

Verify dependency score direction, dynamic range, controls, missingness, and quality flags separately for each source. Avoid direct subtraction of Chronos values from Sanger fold-change or essentiality scores.

Preserve the seven-model environmental design as a small, selected experiment. Determine whether row-level paired outcomes, replicates, and required metadata are sufficient for the intended contrast. A batch correction cannot separate laboratory and biology when the experimental design confounds them.

Record attrition explicitly, including models unable to grow in a condition and genes excluded for insufficient variation or precision.

Data eligibility is determined from prespecified quality and identifiability rules. It must not be selected later to maximize a favorable result.

The gate is an identifiable, falsifiable question in a named subset. If the data cannot support the original question, propose and document a narrower still-open question within the same scientific objective. Do not pretend the original broader goal has been achieved.


6 - Scientific estimands and research contract

Start from two connected estimands.

The direct environmental estimand is the change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and environment B within the same biological model, while holding the other measured culture condition fixed. Analyze medium and growth format separately where the design permits.

The transfer estimand is the extent to which a prespecified genotype, tumor-state, or context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline.

Use original documented assay units for paired within-study contrasts. Use justified ranking, calibration, or outcome-specific comparisons for cross-study evidence.

For each environmental claim, report the dependency in both conditions, all individual paired changes, uncertainty, and heterogeneity. A near-zero average can conceal large opposing effects.

Do not call a dependency invariant because a difference is nonsignificant. If claiming equivalence, define a scientifically justified equivalence margin before confirmatory evaluation and require the relevant interval to fall inside that margin. If no defensible margin exists, report continuous effects and uncertainty without an invariance label.

A stable absence of dependency is not a stable cancer vulnerability. Establish that the dependency itself is meaningful in the environments covered by a positive claim.

Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.

Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.

During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.

The research contract must include the following.

- Exact question and falsifiable alternatives.
- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
- Data and identifier versions.
- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
- Feature engineering and transformation rules.
- Primary and secondary endpoints with fixed direction.
- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.
- Multiplicity control and uncertainty methods.
- Baseline definitions.
- Invalid-result rules.
- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.

The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.

Hash this contract, evaluator code, and split manifests. Changing a locked scientific contract creates a new study identifier and requires appropriate new evaluation evidence.


7 - Novelty and biological scope

Maintain a claim ledger throughout the project.

For each proposed claim, record the nearest prior result, which part is already established, the precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed to resolve it, and the consequence if the claim is supported or refuted.

Read the Broad environmental analyses, the Sanger biomarker analyses, and the October transfer study closely. Their reported lipid, adhesion, state, and dependency findings are controls or prior evidence. Recovering them is not a new discovery.

Use current primary literature, author data, and official documentation. Date the search and preserve queries and important exclusions. Absence of an exact search hit is not proof that a claim is new.

Choose a bounded hypothesis family before final evaluation. Consider gene classes with measured environmental sensitivity and adequate overlap, not an unconstrained scan of every feature until a small p-value appears.

Before finalizing the manuscript, repeat the novelty audit for each surviving exact claim. Preserve contradictions and prior evidence. Do not silently rename a known association to make it appear new.

The required scientific contribution must be more specific than weak organoid transfer, better average correlation, or successful agent automation.


8 - Baselines, inference, and adversarial checks

Build simple competent baselines before allowing elaborate models.

Before the final lock, reproduce baselines only on development data or independent control data. Any reproduction requiring final external outcomes must occur after the final hypotheses, predictions, artifacts, and inferential code are committed, alongside the final evaluation. Those results cannot inform revisions to the tested study.

Where applicable compare lineage only, genotype only, tumor-state or expression only, genotype plus state, measured context or assay covariates, and a regularized linear predictor modeled on the existing transfer study.

Use the same eligible observations and permitted information for each comparison. Learn preprocessing, feature selection, dimension reduction, and tuning within development folds. Do not optimize a feature representation on final external outcomes.

Compare against the strongest applicable baseline, not only the weakest. If environmental information is central to the explanation, include a prespecified ablation removing it.

Use effect sizes and calibrated predictive or uncertainty endpoints as the scientific evaluator's optimization target. Do not reward p-value reduction, the number of discoveries, manuscript length, or confidence of the LLM explanation.

12 - Completion criteria

Mark the ambitious goal complete only when every applicable criterion below has documentary evidence.

C1 - A current novelty audit identifies a precise unresolved cancer dependency question and distinguishes it from the August resources and October transfer study.

C2 - All analyses use legitimately accessible public data, with complete provenance, licensing, identifier crosswalks, and reproducible retrieval or documented local cache installation.

C3 - The intended paired contrast or conditional relationship is identifiable within a named population. Confounding and selection are disclosed, and independent biological units are counted correctly.

C4 - The scientific contract, eligibility rules, splits, baseline set, practical effects, evaluator, and confirmatory procedures were frozen before adaptive optimization and final evaluation.

C5 - The pipeline reproduces relevant published or author-code baseline results closely enough to establish outcome direction, cohort integrity, and measurement interpretation, with discrepancies resolved or explicitly bounded.

C6 - At least one previously unreported explanatory finding passes the frozen scientific criteria and relevant external tests. A supported refutation or informative bound may qualify when it resolves a meaningful predeclared uncertainty. Mere nonsignificance, a rediscovered association, or a better exploratory score does not qualify.

C7 - The evidence atlas covers all eligible genes in the controlled panel and lists every exclusion. Every entry distinguishes dependency strength, environmental change, uncertainty, heterogeneity, biomarker evidence, transfer evidence, and prior knowledge.

C8 - At least two real policy-improvement cycles have run, with executable challengers, faithful replay, fresh rollout evaluation, retained failed attempts, and a final frozen-policy comparison on reserved panels.

C9 - The learned policy meets the preregistered practical improvement criterion against the strongest fixed baseline under matched total resources. Meeting only a deployment-only criterion does not satisfy an end-to-end claim.

C10 - Scientific conclusions survive the prespecified multiplicity correction, appropriate uncertainty analysis, key ablations, and adversarial checks. Any claim of invariance requires positive dependency evidence and justified equivalence evidence.

C11 - A clean reproduction run regenerates the primary estimates, figures, evidence decisions, and policy comparison within documented tolerances from frozen artifacts. It must not require rerunning stochastic LLM search to reproduce the reported analysis.

C12 - The package includes a manuscript for expert review, machine-readable evidence, source and data manifests, all experiment histories, model and policy versions, actual resource measurements, failures, limitations, and exact reproduce and resume commands.

C13 - The first meaningful end-to-end research cycle has actually executed. A plan, scaffold, mock evaluator, or hypothetical example is insufficient.

Seek multiple convergent findings across adequately represented lineages and pathways as an additional scientific stretch target. Do not replace one strong finding with a quota of weak associations. Do not force claims about sparsely represented cancer types.

Keep scientific discovery, prediction improvement, and policy improvement as separate status fields. One does not establish the others.


13 - Required artifacts and commands

Create a compact repository with equivalent artifacts to the following. Choose actual file formats suited to the content.

- README.md with scope, installation, exact commands, and current limitations.
- GOAL.md with the locked goal and completion criteria.
- PROTOCOL.md and a machine-readable locked research contract.
- STATUS.md and a structured status record with current phase, incumbent, spending, next action, and unmet gates.
- A data manifest, source manifest, donor/model crosswalk, eligibility registry, and split manifests.
- A novelty and claim ledger with the complete decision history.
- Data adapters, baseline models, a fixed evaluator, policy modules, a faithful replay engine, a local runner, and a budget wrapper.
- An append-only experiment ledger and immutable discovery artifacts.
- Frozen final predictions, scientific models, policies, and checksums.
- An evidence atlas in a machine-readable table.
- Figures, a manuscript draft, a limitations statement, and an adversarial-review report.
- Environment and dependency locks, licenses and attribution, and a reproducibility report.
- Meaningful tests for identity leakage, score direction, grouping, estimator behavior, cost accounting, resume idempotence, unsupported replay actions, and final-outcome access.

Provide simple documented commands equivalent to bootstrap, audit-data, reproduce-baselines, run-pilot, run-cycle, evaluate-policy, lock-science, evaluate-external, reproduce, status, and resume.

Do not overbuild a general agent platform before the first valid scientific vertical slice works. Reuse maintained components where that reduces risk, but inspect their resource demands and permissions.


14 - Terminal outcomes and reporting

Use explicit terminal states.

- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_DISCOVERY
- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_REFUTATION_OR_BOUND
- SCIENTIFIC_RESULT_WITHOUT_RSI_EVIDENCE
- RSI_RESULT_WITHOUT_SCIENTIFIC_DISCOVERY
- INCONCLUSIVE
- BLOCKED
- BUDGET_EXHAUSTED

The first two require every relevant completion gate, including demonstrated policy improvement. The others are honest outcomes and must list which parts of the ambitious goal remain unmet.

A completed computational campaign is not automatically a completed scientific goal. Do not relabel insufficient power as equivalence or a polished manuscript as a discovery.

When blocked or budget-limited, checkpoint all valid work, identify the exact missing condition, and provide the smallest concrete next step. Do not lower scientific standards, spend beyond the cap, or retry indefinitely.

During execution, report concise milestones with measured results, unresolved risks, and the next discriminating experiment. Avoid progress theater based on file counts or unexecuted plans.

## Environmental panel (Broad minipool) — corrected counts
- 18 screens in released Chronos matrix (18×1000, 0 missing) from **34 sequencing attempts** (35 rows in sequence metadata incl. pDNA reference); 16/18 screens attempted with technical duplicates; **1 replicate removed** (`0178_Adh_Nat_Serum_Dome_RepB`, low NNMD) — screen retained on remaining replicate. Observation-level table: `data/observation_provenance_minipool.csv` (35 rows: model, lineage where known, lab=Broad, library, medium, format, replicate, reads, in-matrix flag, attrition notes).
- **Contrast-specific independent support (corrected):** medium bundle (OPAC vs RPMI/FBS) = 5 pairs / **4 distinct models**; format bundle (Matrigel dome vs plastic) = 7 pairs / **6 distinct models**. `ACH-003040` contributes 2 pairs to each (only complete 4-condition model) — equal-pair weighting overweights it; Lock 1 defines model-level weighting. **Donor counts UNRESOLVED** for all 7 models (no donor field in released metadata); models ≠ verified independent donors.
- Condition coverage per model is sparse (2–4 of 4 cells); released metadata does **not** distinguish unattempted vs growth-failed vs QC-excluded for missing cells → recorded as "not in released matrix; reason unavailable". Zero matrix missingness ≠ complete experimental coverage.
- **Treatment bundles (named honestly):** medium = full OPAC↔RPMI/FBS composition change (not isolated serum); format = plastic↔Matrigel-dome (geometry + matrix exposure, not isolated geometry). Interpretations restricted to observed within-model bundle contrasts.
- **QC discrepancy in primary source (documented, unresolved):** S1 Methods state ≥500,000 reads across replicates; Results/Fig. 5 text states ≥5×10⁶ total readcounts + NNMD ≤ −1.5. Observed screen reads (6.7M–59M) satisfy both; no analysis impact identified, recorded for fidelity.

## Statistics — retraction
v1's "heterogeneity dominates / averages near-uninterpretable" is **retracted**. Sign-flip rates for test genes (medium 90.8%, format 98.4%) match 750 controls (92.1%, 98.1%) and zero-effect noise expectations. Magnitude evidence: median |Δ| medium 0.183 (test) vs 0.164 (controls); mean |mean Δ| 0.137 vs 0.094 — modestly larger test-gene effects, consistent with selection, not proof of heterogeneity. All numbers regenerable via `src/audit_minipool.py` → `data/identifiability_audit.json` (audit v2).

## Selection trace & circularity control
- Library (250 genes) designed from functional classes + the genome-wide 3D-vs-2D comparison: "200 outlier genes" = top 100 each tail of mean dependency difference excluding common essentials (S1 Fig. 5), enriched gene-set clusters then informed classes.

- Residual pathway/class-level selection cannot be excluded per-gene. **All claims are conditional on the fixed selected panel; no generalization to unselected genes.** Broad-side associations are development/descriptive evidence only.

## Preprocessing dependency graphs (audited from source text/code)
- **Broad genome-wide Chronos:** all NextGen screens used Humagne library → library batch **confounded** with organoid-ness; authors pre-trained Chronos on traditional + NextGen **CNS** models, then applied parameters to organoids. Consequence: NextGen Chronos values (incl. the organoids-vs-2D table, n=94 vs 433) are not outcome-independent of NextGen data; usable as **development** evidence, never as independent validation of Broad-derived selections. Minipool Chronos computed "in the same manner" on enAsCas12a screens (separate library); within-model paired contrasts share processing within pairs.
- **Sanger:** pipeline = CRISPRcleanR (per-sample CN correction) → spline normalization (expression bias) → **ComBat batch correction across samples** → BAGEL2 binary calls (verified in `Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R`). ComBat/spline parameters are fitted across the cohort's outcomes → processed Sanger matrices are **not** development-independent by construction. Authoritative processed route per S2: Cell Model Passports + DepMap Miner; figshare holds figure-reproduction files incl. `07_EssMatrix_qnorm_corrected_logFCs.RData` (continuous LFCs; **not downloaded**) and BAGEL binary depletions (downloaded, quarantined; 17,647×930 ACH — a DepMap-wide binary matrix, **not** itself the 162-organoid continuous outcome). Confirmatory use requires, at Lock 1/2: cohort identity via crosswalk, duplicate-library rule (2 libraries/model in places), and either development-only refit from counts (`sgRNAs-counts…csv` exists on figshare) or explicit downgrade of independence claims. Binary BAGEL ≠ continuous rank endpoint; never substituted silently.
- Assay scales (Chronos vs corrected LFC) remain non-interchangeable; cross-study = rank/calibration only.

"""Exploratory precision feasibility v2 (regenerates data/precision_feasibility_v2.json).

Corrections vs v1 (G1 review r2): lability = model-equal-weighted mean of
|within-pair deltas| (no cancellation inside ACH-003040); partial Spearman
defined EXACTLY as Pearson correlation of rank residuals; eligibility adds
AdherentN>=400 (variable reference support); TMOD excluded from primary
(unadjudicated alias TMOD1/7111; sensitivity includes it). EXPLORATORY ONLY.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.stats import spearmanr

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
rng = np.random.default_rng(20261028)


def lability(d: pd.DataFrame, model_draw: list[str] | None = None) -> pd.Series:
    by_model: dict[str, list[int]] = {}
    for i, c in enumerate(d.columns):
        by_model.setdefault(c.split("|")[0], []).append(i)
    models = model_draw or sorted(by_model)
    per = [d.iloc[:, by_model[m]].abs().mean(axis=1) for m in models]  # mean |delta| within model
    return pd.DataFrame(per).T.mean(axis=1)  # equal weight per model (draw counts multiplicities)


def partial_spearman(y: np.ndarray, x: np.ndarray, z: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Pearson correlation of rank residuals (exact operation, locked here)."""
    def resid(a, b):
        ra, rb = pd.Series(a).rank().values, pd.Series(b).rank().values
        X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(rb), rb])
        beta, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(X, ra, rcond=None)
        return ra - X @ beta
    return float(np.corrcoef(resid(y, z), resid(x, z))[0, 1])


def main() -> dict:
    d = pd.read_csv(ROOT / "data" / "paired_deltas_format.csv", index_col=0)
    tests = pd.read_csv(ROOT / "data/raw/broad_figshare/organoids_vs_2d_gene_dependency_tests_full.csv")
    tests["symbol"] = tests.Dependency.str.replace(r"\s*\(\d+\)$", "", regex=True)
    excl = json.loads((ROOT / "data/selection_exclusions.json").read_text())
    lib = pd.read_csv(ROOT / "data/raw/broad_figshare/minipool_library_composition.csv")
    panel = set(lib.loc[lib["All test genes"] == True, "Gene"])  # noqa: E712
    tidx = tests.set_index("symbol")
    base_elig = sorted(g for g in panel - set(excl["tierA_direct_outliers_excluded"]) if g in tidx.index and g in d.index)
    primary = [g for g in base_elig if tidx.loc[g, "AdherentN"] >= 400 and tidx.loc[g, "OrganoidsN"] >= 90]
    low_n = sorted(set(base_elig) - set(primary))
    out = {"status": "EXPLORATORY feasibility v2; not confirmatory", "primary_genes_n": len(primary),
           "excluded_low_reference_N": low_n, "tmod": "excluded primary (alias TMOD1/7111 unadjudicated from released guide data); sensitivity only"}
    for label, genes in [("primary", primary), ("sensitivity_plus_TMOD1_alias", primary + (["TMOD"] if "TMOD" in d.index else []))]:
        gg = [g for g in genes if (g in tidx.index or g == "TMOD")]
        L = lability(d).loc[[g for g in gg if g in d.index]]
        rows = []
        for g in L.index:
            src = "TMOD1" if g == "TMOD" else g
            if src in tidx.index:
                rows.append((g, L[g], abs(tidx.loc[src, "MeanDifference"]), abs(tidx.loc[src, "AdherentMean"])))
        df = pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=["gene", "Lval", "Tval", "base"]).set_index("gene")
        rho = float(spearmanr(df.Lval, df.Tval).statistic)
        prho = partial_spearman(df.Tval.values, df.Lval.values, df.base.values)
        models = sorted({c.split("|")[0] for c in d.columns})
        boots = []
        for _ in range(2000):
            Lb = lability(d, list(rng.choice(models, size=len(models), replace=True))).loc[df.index]
            boots.append(partial_spearman(df.Tval.values, Lb.values, df.base.values))
        ci = np.percentile([b for b in boots if np.isfinite(b)], [2.5, 97.5]).tolist()
        out[label] = {"n": int(len(df)), "raw_spearman": rho, "partial_spearman_rankresid_pearson": prho,
                      "partial_model_bootstrap_ci95": ci, "ci_width": float(ci[1] - ci[0])}
    out["caveats"] = ("Model bootstrap (6 format models) captures environmental-side uncertainty only; gene/pathway dependence and "
                      "transfer-table sampling uncertainty widen true CIs. Broad table = development/descriptive (selection source, Chronos pretraining). "
                      "External precision (148 organoids/144 donors) computed at Lock 1 from schema + donor counts, not outcomes.")
    (ROOT / "data" / "precision_feasibility_v2.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2))
    print(json.dumps(out, indent=1)[:1800])
    return out


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# Novelty audit v1 — 2026-10-28 (Phase 1f; refresh required at G7 + terminal determination)

## Method
Full-text reads (Europe PMC XML, `data/papers/`): S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187. Dated Europe PMC searches 2026-10-28 (queries preserved in session record): culture/CRISPR/dependency/organoid (7 hits), culture-conditions transfer (14 hits), medium/essentiality 2025–2026 (176 hits, top reviewed). **Absence of a search hit is not proof of novelty** — claims below are candidates pending G1 review.

## Already established (controls / priors — NOT novelty)
1. **S1 (Broad NextGen):** class-level environmental effects in the 250-gene minipool — integrin/actin classes vary by **format** (plastic vs dome), lipid-metabolism class by **medium** (RPMI-FBS vs OPAC), via OLS on 18 screens/7 models. Also: 147 genome-scale NextGen screens, biomarker dependencies, CDK6/CDKN2A-GBM work.
2. **S2 (Sanger biobank):** 162-organoid dependency map, biomarker associations, organoid-specific essentials.
3. **S3 (Oct 2026 transfer study):** expression-only Elastic Net (20 genes) transfer quantification 2D→organoids (Broad-94 ρ=0.15, Sanger-148 ρ=0.10), beyond-lineage checks. **Explicitly did not disentangle** state/geometry/medium/lineage/platform contributions. Generic transfer prediction is therefore closed as a novelty claim.
4. Adjacent: murine MYC 3D-vs-2D fitness programs (PMC12055005, 2025) — different system, no human dependency-transfer estimand.

## Residual uncertainty (the gap)
Nobody has tested whether **measured** per-gene environmental sensitivity (paired medium/format deltas with proper joint uncertainty and heterogeneity) **explains which dependencies fail to transfer** — i.e., whether environment-labile genes are exactly where 2D→organoid relationships (dependency levels and/or biomarker associations) degrade, beyond lineage and baseline strength. S1 measured sensitivity (7 models, class-level report); S3 measured transfer failure (20 genes, no environmental attribution). The link, with correct independent-unit counting and heterogeneity-aware estimands, is unreported in the searched literature.

Our audit adds a sharpening fact: per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity (91–98% of test genes flip sign across model pairs). Any credible claim must therefore be **conditional** (which genes × which model contexts) — class-average or mean-effect versions are likely unidentifiable/misleading at n=7 models. Precision-supported **refutations/bounds** (e.g., "average environmental sensitivity does not explain transfer degradation beyond X") are acceptable G1 targets.

## Candidate claim ledger (exploratory; Lock 1 will bound the family)
- **CL-1 (primary candidate):** Among the 250 panel genes, transfer degradation of dependency behavior (traditional→NextGen organoid, Layer-A genome-wide data) increases with measured environmental sensitivity magnitude/heterogeneity, conditional on lineage + baseline dependency strength. *Falsifier:* no association (or reversed) under joint-uncertainty propagation and strongest-baseline controls. *Needs:* Layer-A genome-wide import, joint bootstrap, matched controls.
- **CL-2 (conditional form):** The CL-1 association is concentrated in specific gene classes/contexts (e.g., medium-labile lipid genes vs format-labile adhesion genes behave differently in transfer). *Risk:* n=7 models ⇒ classes, not genes, may be the finest honest resolution.
- **CL-3 (bound/refutation candidate):** After conditioning, environmental sensitivity explains < δ of transfer degradation (informative bound redirecting interpretation toward lineage/state/platform). Qualifies only with predeclared δ + sufficient precision.
- **Excluded as novelty:** re-deriving S1 class effects; re-running S3's 20-gene transfer; any average-effect "environment matters" statement without heterogeneity/conditioning.

## Open items for G1 gate
S1/S2 supplement + author-code deep read; Zenodo S3 code inspection; Layer-A genome-wide route decision; crosswalk completion; sol-high review of this audit + provenance (G1 gate requires ≥ 90).
