> SUPERSEDED (2026-10-28) by v3/v2 successor. Retained for audit trail.

# Novelty audit v1 — 2026-10-28 (Phase 1f; refresh required at G7 + terminal determination)

## Method
Full-text reads (Europe PMC XML, `data/papers/`): S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187. Dated Europe PMC searches 2026-10-28 (queries preserved in session record): culture/CRISPR/dependency/organoid (7 hits), culture-conditions transfer (14 hits), medium/essentiality 2025–2026 (176 hits, top reviewed). **Absence of a search hit is not proof of novelty** — claims below are candidates pending G1 review.

## Already established (controls / priors — NOT novelty)
1. **S1 (Broad NextGen):** class-level environmental effects in the 250-gene minipool — integrin/actin classes vary by **format** (plastic vs dome), lipid-metabolism class by **medium** (RPMI-FBS vs OPAC), via OLS on 18 screens/7 models. Also: 147 genome-scale NextGen screens, biomarker dependencies, CDK6/CDKN2A-GBM work.
2. **S2 (Sanger biobank):** 162-organoid dependency map, biomarker associations, organoid-specific essentials.
3. **S3 (Oct 2026 transfer study):** expression-only Elastic Net (20 genes) transfer quantification 2D→organoids (Broad-94 ρ=0.15, Sanger-148 ρ=0.10), beyond-lineage checks. **Explicitly did not disentangle** state/geometry/medium/lineage/platform contributions. Generic transfer prediction is therefore closed as a novelty claim.
4. Adjacent: murine MYC 3D-vs-2D fitness programs (PMC12055005, 2025) — different system, no human dependency-transfer estimand.

## Residual uncertainty (the gap)
Nobody has tested whether **measured** per-gene environmental sensitivity (paired medium/format deltas with proper joint uncertainty and heterogeneity) **explains which dependencies fail to transfer** — i.e., whether environment-labile genes are exactly where 2D→organoid relationships (dependency levels and/or biomarker associations) degrade, beyond lineage and baseline strength. S1 measured sensitivity (7 models, class-level report); S3 measured transfer failure (20 genes, no environmental attribution). The link, with correct independent-unit counting and heterogeneity-aware estimands, is unreported in the searched literature.

Our audit adds a sharpening fact: per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity (91–98% of test genes flip sign across model pairs). Any credible claim must therefore be **conditional** (which genes × which model contexts) — class-average or mean-effect versions are likely unidentifiable/misleading at n=7 models. Precision-supported **refutations/bounds** (e.g., "average environmental sensitivity does not explain transfer degradation beyond X") are acceptable G1 targets.

## Candidate claim ledger (exploratory; Lock 1 will bound the family)
- **CL-1 (primary candidate):** Among the 250 panel genes, transfer degradation of dependency behavior (traditional→NextGen organoid, Layer-A genome-wide data) increases with measured environmental sensitivity magnitude/heterogeneity, conditional on lineage + baseline dependency strength. *Falsifier:* no association (or reversed) under joint-uncertainty propagation and strongest-baseline controls. *Needs:* Layer-A genome-wide import, joint bootstrap, matched controls.
- **CL-2 (conditional form):** The CL-1 association is concentrated in specific gene classes/contexts (e.g., medium-labile lipid genes vs format-labile adhesion genes behave differently in transfer). *Risk:* n=7 models ⇒ classes, not genes, may be the finest honest resolution.
- **CL-3 (bound/refutation candidate):** After conditioning, environmental sensitivity explains < δ of transfer degradation (informative bound redirecting interpretation toward lineage/state/platform). Qualifies only with predeclared δ + sufficient precision.
- **Excluded as novelty:** re-deriving S1 class effects; re-running S3's 20-gene transfer; any average-effect "environment matters" statement without heterogeneity/conditioning.

## Open items for G1 gate
S1/S2 supplement + author-code deep read; Zenodo S3 code inspection; Layer-A genome-wide route decision; crosswalk completion; sol-high review of this audit + provenance (G1 gate requires ≥ 90).

"""Phase-1 identifiability audit v2 — fully regenerates every reported number.

Corrections vs v1 (sol-high G1 review): contrast-specific DISTINCT model
counts (medium 4, format 6; ACH-003040 contributes 2 pairs each); sign-flip
rates reported for test genes AND 750 controls with NO heterogeneity claim
(sign flips at control rates prove little — magnitudes are the evidence);
direction check vs published OLS coefficients computed here, not pasted.
Descriptive audit only — no adaptive search, no claims.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
RAW = ROOT / "data" / "raw" / "broad_figshare"


def paired_deltas(meta, eff, contrast):
    rows = {}
    if contrast == "medium":  # OPAC - RPMI/FBS within model+format (treatment BUNDLE: full medium change)
        groups, key, a, b = meta.groupby(["DepMap ID", "Growth Format"]), "Culture medium", "OPAC", "RPMI/FBS"
    else:  # Dome - Plastic within model+medium (BUNDLE: geometry + Matrigel matrix exposure)
        groups, key, a, b = meta.groupby(["DepMap ID", "Culture medium"]), "Growth Format", "Dome", "Plastic"
    for gkey, g in groups:
        ga, gb = g[g[key] == a], g[g[key] == b]
        if len(ga) == 1 and len(gb) == 1:
            rows[f"{gkey[0]}|{gkey[1]}"] = eff.loc[ga.cell_line_name.iloc[0]] - eff.loc[gb.cell_line_name.iloc[0]]
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


def _stats(x: pd.DataFrame) -> dict:
    return {
        "both_signs_frac": float(((x > 0).any(axis=1) & (x < 0).any(axis=1)).mean()),
        "note": "sign-flip rate is DESCRIPTIVE ONLY (controls flip at similar rates; not evidence of biological heterogeneity)",
        "median_abs_delta": float(x.abs().median().median()),
        "mean_abs_mean_delta": float(x.mean(axis=1).abs().mean()),
        "frac_genes_abs_mean_delta_gt_0.5": float((x.mean(axis=1).abs() > 0.5).mean()),
    }


def main() -> dict:
    meta = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_screen_metadata.csv")
    eff = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_chronos_gene_effects.csv").set_index("ScreenID")
    lib = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_library_composition.csv")
    test = set(lib.loc[lib["All test genes"] == True, "Gene"])  # noqa: E712
    out: dict = {"audit_version": 2, "n_screens": int(len(meta)), "n_models_total": int(meta["DepMap ID"].nunique()),
                 "models": sorted(meta["DepMap ID"].unique()), "n_test_genes": len(test),
                 "treatment_bundles": {"medium": "OPAC (serum-free defined) vs RPMI/FBS — full medium-composition bundle, NOT isolated serum",
                                        "format": "Matrigel dome vs plastic — geometry + matrix-exposure bundle, NOT isolated geometry"}}
    coef = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_regression_coefficients.csv", index_col=0)
    pub = {"format": -coef.loc["C(Growth_Format)[T.Plastic]"], "medium": coef.loc["C(Serum_status)[T.Serum-free]"]}
    for contrast in ("medium", "format"):
        d = paired_deltas(meta, eff, contrast)
        d.to_csv(ROOT / "data" / f"paired_deltas_{contrast}.csv")
        distinct = sorted({c.split("|")[0] for c in d.columns})
        entry = {"n_pairs": int(d.shape[1]), "pair_keys": list(d.columns),
                 "n_distinct_models": len(distinct), "distinct_models": distinct,
                 "donors": "UNRESOLVED (crosswalk open; do not equate models with independent donors)",
                 "double_counted_model": "ACH-003040 contributes 2 pairs; equal-pair weighting overweights it — Lock 1 must define model-level weighting",
                 "test250": _stats(d.loc[d.index.intersection(test)]),
                 "controls750": _stats(d.loc[~d.index.isin(test)])}
        ours = d.mean(axis=1)
        j = pd.DataFrame({"ours": ours, "pub": pub[contrast]}).dropna()
        jt = j.loc[j.index.intersection(test)]
        entry["direction_check_vs_published_OLS"] = {
            "test250_pearson": float(jt.ours.corr(jt.pub)), "test250_spearman": float(jt.ours.corr(jt.pub, method="spearman")),
            "test250_sign_agreement": float((np.sign(jt.ours) == np.sign(jt.pub)).mean()),
            "all1000_pearson": float(j.ours.corr(j.pub)),
            "note": "format sign flipped (ours Dome-Plastic vs published Plastic indicator). C5 precursor only."}
        out[contrast] = entry
    out["retracted_v1_claim"] = ("v1 'heterogeneity dominates / averages near-uninterpretable' RETRACTED: control genes flip signs at "
                                  "equal rates. Evidence must come from magnitudes vs controls/technical variation.")
    (ROOT / "data" / "identifiability_audit.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2))
    print(json.dumps({k: out[k] for k in ("medium", "format")}, indent=1)[:2200])
    return out


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

PLAN.md:23:- **Context/retrieval contract:** "exhaustive/full history" always means **exhaustive over the episode's admissible history** (§0.4.2 filter) — the same filter applies to *every* input channel: history index, retrieved records, rendered prompts, summaries, priorities, and unstick packets. Proposer inputs (token-bounded by construction; budgets frozen in the SPEC with reserved space for instructions + retrieved records): (a) **bounded directory header** — partition list with counts and ID ranges only (never a full ID dump), (b) **history index partitions** (fixed-size rows: ID, direction, origin, parents, outcome class, quality, cost), partitioned deterministically by direction and **sub-partitioned by fixed row/token caps so no partition — including one huge direction — exceeds its bound**, (c) episode-admissible catalog/frontier view, (d) direction summary, (e) full records via a **runner-mediated ID lookup** (proposer cites IDs or ID ranges; runner resolves partition→records deterministically, budgeted per generation, every lookup logged; cross-partition access always by ID). Complete addressability = every admissible ID is reachable via header→partition→lookup, without ever rendering the whole directory. **Overflow outcome:** if no legal rendering fits the frozen token budget, the generation call splits further (finer sub-partitions, header unchanged) — if still infeasible, the SPEC must narrow its index scope explicitly and the narrowing is ledgered; silent truncation is forbidden. Fixtures required: directory-alone-exceeds-cap and single-direction-exceeds-cap cases both rendering legally. What each call received is recorded in its input manifest. This is the single definition of "full tried history" used in GOAL.md. The hidden-outcome invariance test (§0.4.2) must cover the **complete rendered input and resulting decisions**, all channels included.

**Recommend one fixed, panel-wide association using the six-model format contrast and independently evaluated molecular-prediction loss.** It is potentially G1-passable after resolving data access and preprocessing provenance. **Nothing currently establishes a G1 pass**, and an imprecise null would not rescue the gate.

The [review’s three critical failures](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/reviews/g1_review_r1_verdict.md:1) require an operational endpoint, separation from selection outcomes, and an audited processing dependency graph.

1. **Preferred: format lability versus external prediction loss.** Retains the scientific connection between controlled environmental measurements and molecular transfer. Requires individual-level conventional-model data, Sanger continuous outcomes and molecular covariates, and the provenance audit described below.
2. **Matrix-saving alternative: transfer of a fixed 2D consensus target ordering.** Use `AdherentMean` as the frozen source ranking and evaluate its ordering errors across genes within Sanger organoids. This avoids fitting conventional-model predictors. It is a different question: consensus target prioritization, with no claim about loss of genotype/expression relationships or improvement beyond S3’s molecular baseline.
3. **Processing fallback: independent public measurements or pre-joint-correction processed outputs.** Retain the first route’s estimand but replace evidence whose processing crosses the development/evaluation boundary. This remains conditional on locating suitable public observations; “reprocess independently” is not a concrete solution when required inputs are unavailable.

A correlation between minipool effects and the downloaded Broad group-comparison table is useful development evidence. It cannot clear the independent-transfer gate.

**1. One primary estimand for the preferred route**

Choose **format**, based on its six model supports. The intervention bundle is **plastic culture versus Matrigel-dome culture, holding the recorded medium fixed**. It does not isolate geometry from matrix exposure.

For gene \(g\), define measured format lability:

\[
s_g=\frac{1}{6}\sum_{m=1}^{6}
       \frac{1}{|K_m|}\sum_{k\in K_m}
       \left|Y_{mgk,\mathrm{Dome}}-Y_{mgk,\mathrm{Plastic}}\right|.
\]

Here \(K_m\) contains the observed matched media. ACH-003040’s two medium-specific contrasts receive half its model weight each; every other model contributes one contrast. Each model therefore receives weight \(1/6\).

The target family is the **224 test genes outside the exact 200-gene selection tail**, subsequently restricted only by frozen identifier, assay-coverage and source-estimability rules. This is one selected family, with equal target weights.

For transfer:

- Train a molecular dependency predictor \(f_g\) using conventional models only.
- Reserve conventional reference donors \(R\) disjoint from training donors \(T\).
- Apply the **same fitted predictor** to \(R\) and donor-disjoint Sanger organoids \(C\).
- Evaluate within colorectal, oesophageal, gastric, ovarian and pancreatic groups, with equal prespecified group weights. Required source coverage must be demonstrated before locking these groups.
- Retain one sample/library per donor using an outcome-independent selection hierarchy.

For domain \(D\in\{R,C\}\), define target-specific donor-ordering accuracy:

\[
C_g^D=\frac15\sum_{\ell=1}^{5}
 P\!\left[
 (f_g(X_i)-f_g(X_j))(Y_{ig}-Y_{jg})>0
 \mid i,j\text{ distinct donors in group }\ell
 \right],
\]

with ties receiving half credit. Then define transfer loss:

\[
d_g=C_g^R-C_g^C.
\]

Positive values mean worse ordering in organoids. This compares ordering accuracy without subtracting Chronos from Sanger LFC.

The **single primary parameter** is the finite-panel linear-projection coefficient

\[
\boxed{\displaystyle
\beta=
\frac{a^\top M_Xd}{a^\top M_Xa},
\qquad a_g=s_g/\operatorname{IQR}(s).
}
\]

\(M_X\) removes the fixed baseline covariates: source dependency mean and SD, source ordering accuracy, signed and absolute Broad selection-table `MeanDifference`, and the library’s functional-class indicators. Use their fixed column space; overlapping classes do not justify adaptive variable selection.

**Interpretation:** additional loss of correct donor ordering per one-IQR increase in measured format lability, conditional on those baselines, this target family and these environmental supports.

The predictor comparison must include lineage-only, genotype-only, S3-style expression Elastic Net, and genotype-plus-expression models, using the same permitted observations. Select the predictor family and tune it entirely within \(T\), then freeze it before evaluating \(R\) and \(C\). Environmental scores enter the explanatory association, not external predictor selection.

This route still needs the individual-level source matrix. Published group means cannot supply \(C_g^R\).

**2. Selection circularity: exact exclusion plus conditional external evaluation**

The downloaded trace supports this reproducible rule:

1. In `organoids_vs_2d_gene_dependency_tests_full.csv`, retain `DropEssential=False`.
2. Sort by signed `MeanDifference`.
3. Take the lowest 100 and highest 100 genes. **Do not substitute an FDR cutoff.**
4. Match the table’s `SYMBOL (EntrezID)` identifiers to library genes.
5. Exclude their intersection with `All test genes=True`.

That removes **26 of 250 test genes**, leaving **224** before other eligibility exclusions. The tail boundaries have no ties.

However, **224 does not mean selection-independent genes**. Whole functional classes were chosen using the same comparison. The [library documentation](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/raw/broad_figshare/README.txt:1) and enrichment trace make that pathway-level selection explicit.

The remaining protection is therefore:

- Condition the claim on the entire historical panel-selection process and fixed family.
- Use the Broad comparison table only as development information and frozen baseline covariates.
- Obtain the evaluation outcome from Sanger donors excluded from all overlapping Broad environmental, selection, training and reference cohorts.
- Exclude uncertain donor matches from confirmatory eligibility.
- Establish that Sanger evaluation outcomes did not influence any source predictor, selection artifact or environmental-score processing artifact.

This can neutralize **reuse of the selection outcome as confirmation**. It cannot identify a genome-wide, selection-unconditional association. Adjusting for `MeanDifference` alone would not establish independence.

My dispositions of the r2 items follow.

1. **R1-1 — Operational estimand: RESOLVED WITH NARROWING.**

This clears the central r2 objection. “Transfer loss” must mean error in transferring this fixed consensus ordering; there is no source-held-out performance comparison establishing degradation relative to ordinary 2D performance.

   Seven format pairs support six models, with ACH-003040’s two absolute contrasts averaged within its model weight. Six verified independent donors are still unavailable. Conditioning interpretation on the measured models is appropriate.

Consequently, the predictor measures **observed score lability**, potentially including measurement noise; it does not identify latent biological sensitivity.

    The plausible gap is the association between measured format lability and external consensus-ordering displacement. S3 already examined within-organoid ranking and a fixed gene-mean comparator; neither ranking itself nor expanding to 207 genes establishes novelty. The lability–displacement relationship is the defensible remaining contribution. Audit v2 establishes plausibility rather than an exhaustive novelty finding.

12. **R1-12 — Falsification, precision, and δ: PARTIALLY RESOLVED; ACCEPTABLE LOCK-1 WORK REMAINS.**  
    The two-sided BOUND rule is correct, the interval now concerns the specified partial statistic, and external donor counts are demonstrated. However, precision for the external endpoint remains unestablished. The development interval concerns a different response and cannot establish that external displacement is stable or that environmental-side uncertainty dominates. The practical interpretation of δ also remains unsupported.

13. **R1-13 — Manifest completeness and reproducibility: PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    The precision calculation is regenerable, hashes match, code revisions are stated, and the corrected maximum read count **88,316,766** reproduces. But the assertion that every manifest entry now has `article_version=1` is false: **9/13 Broad entries still lack it**. This is a documentation defect rather than an identification failure.

The additional restrictions needed for the narrowed approval are substantive:

- **Baseline adjustment does not remove rank-position effects.** Under completely random external rankings, expected normalized displacement is **0.5** for either extreme of the reference ordering but approximately **0.2512** at its middle. Dividing by `G−1` does not equalize this. Linear residualization on ranked absolute baseline strength cannot generally remove that nonlinear structure. The present statistic identifies its specified partial association; “beyond baseline strength” must carry that precise meaning. A prespecified reference-rank adjustment or null-calibrated sensitivity would test whether the association survives this mechanical explanation.

- **“minLib primary” does not specify a complete 148-organoid analysis.** Among eligible models, **133 have minLib**, **15 have only v1_1**, and **13 have both libraries**. Sixteen dual-library models describes the entire screened resource. A rule preferring minLib when available and otherwise retaining v1_1 would preserve 148 models, but it must be explicit. Restricting exclusively to minLib changes the cohort.

- **The uncertainty target needs reconciliation.** V3 conditions claims on the measured six models while using their bootstrap as primary uncertainty. For a fixed measured-panel estimand, model resampling is a stability analysis; inference to a wider model population needs additional sampling assumptions. Likewise, donor bootstrapping author-processed outcomes does not automatically propagate uncertainty in jointly fitted normalization. These choices must be resolved before locking inference. The frozen `AdherentMean` reference also participates in the confirmatory endpoint, despite the statement that its processing affects only exploratory work.

- **The δ argument does not establish decision relevance.** A partial correlation of rank residuals has no general conversion into Kendall concordance or shortlist utility. Even a valid approximate concordance conversion would not establish that shortlist composition cannot change. Lock 1 can retain δ = 0.20 only with an explicit decision justification, or restrict BOUND wording to the magnitude of this association. A substantial negative association would also deserve explicit reporting as evidence against the positive-direction conjecture.

These limitations narrow interpretation and constrain Lock 1. They do not erase the demonstrated route to the named G1 question. External evaluation, a scientifically informative bound, and the broader completion criteria remain unachieved.

I excluded those values from the verdict’s reasoning. **Table-5/6 dependency values were neither displayed nor analyzed.** The existing exposure log does not include this incident. No files were edited.

This supports a conditional association study. Claims about molecular-predictor degradation, causal environmental explanations, or practical target-triage usefulness remain unestablished.
